<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the regular-point bi-Lipschitz conjecture in the noncollapsed setting. For every integer <i>n</i> ≥ 2 and every real <i>K</i>, every regular point of a noncollapsed $`\mathrm{RCD}(K,n)`$ space has an open neighborhood bi-Lipschitz homeomorphic to an open subset of ℝ<sup><i>n</i></sup>. The bi-Lipschitz constant depends only on <i>n</i>, and the neighborhood uses the restricted ambient distance.

- **C001** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $n\ge2$ there is a constant $L_n\ge1$ with the following property. Let $K\in\mathbb R$, and let $(X,d,\mathcal H_d^n)$ be a noncollapsed $\mathrm{RCD}(K,n)$ space. Let $p\in X$. If every pointed Gromov–Hausdorff tangent of $X$ at $p$ is pointed-isometric to $(\mathbb R^n,\|\cdot\|,0)$, then there exist an open neighborhood $U$ of $p$, an open set $V\subset\mathbb R^n$, and a homeomorphism $F:U\to V$ such that $$L_n^{-1}d(x,z)\le \|F(x)-F(z)\|\le L_nd(x,z) \qquad (x,z\in U).$$ The distance on the left and right is the original distance $d$ restricted to $U$.
  - Explanation: Every prescribed regular point has an ambient-open Euclidean chart with dimension-dependent bi-Lipschitz distortion.
- **C002** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-full-measure-atlas)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, there is an open set $\mathcal U\subset X$ containing every $n$-regular point, with $\mathcal H_d^n(X\setminus\mathcal U)=0$, and a countable atlas of $L_n$-bi-Lipschitz charts from ambient-open subsets of $\mathcal U$ onto open subsets of $\mathbb R^n$. Each chart uses the restricted ambient distance, and its transition maps are $L_n^2$-bi-Lipschitz.
  - Explanation: The chart domains cover an open region of full Hausdorff measure; overlaps have controlled transition distortion.
- **C003** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-local-calculus)): Let $(Y,d,\mathfrak m)$ be a noncollapsed $\mathrm{RCD}(-\kappa,n)$ space and let $\Omega\subset Y$ be open. A locally bounded solution of $\Delta w=a$ in $\Omega$, with $a$ constant, is locally Lipschitz and is a local test function. In particular it has a local $L^2$ Hessian, $\Gamma(w_1,w_2)\in W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)$ for two such functions, and the Hessian product rule holds locally. The measure $$\label{co:M-definition} M(w)=\frac12\Delta\Gamma(w)+\kappa\Gamma(w)\mathfrak m$$ is locally finite and nonnegative. For every constant $b$, $$\label{co:shifted-bochner} M(w)-(2b\Delta w-nb^2)\mathfrak m \ge \|\operatorname{Hess}w-bg\|^2\mathfrak m.$$ On the real vector space of local constant-Laplacian functions, the left side is a nonnegative quadratic form in the pair $(w,b)$, with both entries scaled together. All these statements hold on interior balls for weak solutions with locally bounded $L^2$ norm, after using the interior estimates to obtain local boundedness.* *On fixed interior scales, bounded Lipschitz cutoffs with bounded Laplacian are available. Under uniform local volume comparison and a uniform rescaled curvature bound, their bounds, local Poincare and Sobolev constants, and interior harmonic and Poisson estimates are uniform. In particular, on a fixed-radius ball in the rescaled spaces below there is a number $d_*>1$ and a constant $C$ such that $$\label{co:dirichlet-sobolev} \\|h\\|_{L^2}+\\|h\\|_{L^{2d_*}} \le C\\|\nabla h\\|_{L^2},\qquad h\in W^{1,2}_0(B),$$ whenever the ball and a fixed larger ball are contained in the working region.
  - Explanation: Constant-Laplacian solutions admit local test calculus, a positive shifted Bochner measure, and uniform interior Dirichlet estimates.
- **C004** (construction, [source](#stmt-co-flat-setup)): Fix an $n$-regular point $p$ in the space of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. There are rescalings $$(X_\nu,d_\nu,\mathfrak m_\nu,p_\nu) =(X,s_\nu^{-1}d,s_\nu^{-n}\mathcal H_d^n,p), \qquad s_\nu\downarrow0,$$ numbers $\kappa_\nu\downarrow0$, and radii $T_\nu\uparrow\infty$ with $\kappa_\nu T_\nu^2\to0$, having the following properties. The space $X_\nu$ is $\mathrm{RCD}(-\kappa_\nu,n)$. For a sequence $\delta_\nu\downarrow0$, all balls with center in $B_{64T_\nu}(p_\nu)$ and radius at most $64T_\nu$ satisfy $$\label{co:uniform-flatness} \left\|\frac{\mathfrak m_\nu(B_r(y))}{\omega_n r^n}-1\right\| \le\delta_\nu, \qquad d_{GH}\bigl(\overline B_r(y),\overline B_r(0^n)\bigr) \le\delta_\nu r.$$ There are harmonic columns $u_\nu$ on $B_{32T_\nu}(p_\nu)$, converging on every fixed ball locally uniformly and strongly in $W^{1,2}$ to Euclidean coordinates, and $$\label{co:original-gradient-bound} \|\nabla u_\nu\|\le C_n \quad\text{on }B_{16T_\nu}(p_\nu).$$
  - Explanation: Rescalings around the prescribed regular point provide expanding almost Euclidean regions and harmonic coordinate approximations.
- **C005** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-harmonic-normalization)): For every pair of fixed constants $A,C\ge1$, the matrices $R_i(y)$ are positive definite for $y\in B_A(p)$ and $i\ge0$. In the rescaled ball $B_{Cr_i}(y)$, the map $$\label{co:normalized-column} U_{i,y}=\frac{R_i(y)^{-1/2}(u-u(y))}{r_i}$$ is, after an orthogonal change of target coordinates, an $o(1)$ approximation to Euclidean coordinates, uniformly and strongly in interior $W^{1,2}$. Its gradients are uniformly bounded on each fixed interior enlargement. Its Gram matrix converges strongly in every finite $L^p$ on such enlargements to $\mathrm{Id}$.* *For every fixed $C\ge1$, if $d(y,z)\le Cr_i$ and $C^{-1}r_i\le r_j\le Cr_i$, then $$\label{co:nearby-R} (1-o(1))R_i(y)\le R_j(z)\le(1+o(1))R_i(y).$$ For every $a>0$, after choosing a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\label{co:slow-R} C_a^{-1}2^{-2a\|i-j\|}R_j(y) \le R_i(y)\le C_a2^{2a\|i-j\|}R_j(y).$$ The constants are uniform in the center and the two indices. In particular, since $R_0(y)=\mathrm{Id}+o(1)$ on fixed balls, $$\label{co:R-lower} c_a r_i^{2a}\mathrm{Id}\le R_i(y)\le C\mathrm{Id}.$$
  - Explanation: Mean Gram matrices remain positive definite at every center and scale, with canonical normalized coordinates and slow scale growth.
- **C006** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-bochner-small)): For each pair of fixed constants $A,C\ge1$, uniformly for $y\in B_A(p)$ and $i\ge0$, $$\label{co:small-mass} r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Cr_i}(y))\le o(1)R_i(y).$$
  - Explanation: Normalized local Bochner mass is uniformly small relative to the corresponding mean Gram matrix.
- **C007** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-open-coordinates)): For every fixed $A\ge1$, the map $u$ is a homeomorphism from $B_A(p)$ onto an open subset of $\mathbb R^n$, once the rescaling is sufficiently late. There is a fixed $c>0$ such that, whenever the balls involved stay in a fixed interior working region, $$\label{co:ellipsoid-containment} \{P:\|P-u(x)\|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}<c r_i\} \subset u(B_{r_i/2}(x)).$$ Moreover, for every fixed $D\ge1$, if $D^{-1}r_i\le d(x,z)\le Dr_i$, then $$\label{co:separation-scale} \bigl\|\|u(z)-u(x)\|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}-d(x,z)\bigr\| \le o_D(1)r_i.$$
  - Explanation: Sufficiently late harmonic coordinates are open homeomorphisms, with central ellipsoid containment and scale separation.
- **C008** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-kernel-measures)): Fix $C_0\ge1$, $x,y$ in a fixed testing region, and $d(x,y)\le C_0r$, where $0<r\le1$. Put $$K_t(z)=\mathop{\mathchoice {\displaystyle\int\mkern-18mu-} {\textstyle\int\mkern-15mu-} {\scriptstyle\int\mkern-12mu-} {\scriptscriptstyle\int\mkern-10mu-}}\nolimits_{B_r(y)}p_t(a,z)\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m(a), \qquad D=d(x,z).$$ After enlarging fixed constants, the following bounds hold at every source pole $z$: $$\begin{aligned} K_t(z)&\le C r^{-n}e^{-cD^2/r^2},&&0<t\le r^2, \label{co:short-kernel}\\ \|K_t(z)-p_t(x,z)\|&\le C r t^{-(n+1)/2}e^{-cD^2/t}, &&r^2\le t\le1.\label{co:long-kernel} \end{aligned}$$ If $f\in W^{1,2}\cap L^\infty$ has compact support and its Laplacian is a finite signed measure $\mu$ of compact support, then $$\label{co:semigroup-measure} P_t f-P_s f=\int_s^t P_\tau\mu\,\mathrm d\tau, \qquad 0<s<t,$$ using the continuous representatives. In the application $f=\chi\Gamma(u_j,u_k)$, this identity can also be averaged over a ball and passed to $s\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Continuous heat kernels control averaged spatial differences and represent semigroup evolution for compactly supported measure sources.
- **C009** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-heat-matrices)): There is $\lambda=\lambda_\nu>0$, with $\lambda_\nu\to0$, such that, at all fixed testing centers and $0<t\le1$, $$\label{co:heat-derivative} P_t(\chi M) \le\partial_t\bigl(P_t(\chi G)+\lambda t\mathrm{Id}\bigr) \le 3P_t(\chi M)+C\mathrm{Id}.$$ Define $$\label{co:QH-definition} Q_i(x)=P_{r_i^2}(\chi G)(x)+\lambda r_i^2\mathrm{Id}, \qquad H_i(x)=Q_i(x)^{-1}.$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} &(1-o(1))R_i(x)\le Q_i(x)\le(1+o(1))R_i(x), \qquad Q_0(x)=\mathrm{Id}+o(1),\label{co:QR-comparison}\\ &H_i(x)\le H_{i+1}(x)\le(1+o(1))H_i(x). \label{co:H-monotone} \end{aligned}$$ The nearby-center and slow-growth estimates of Lemma <a href="#co:harmonic-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="co:harmonic-normalization">5</a> hold for $Q_i$ as well. In particular, for every prescribed $a>0$, with a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\label{co:slow-QH} Q_j(x)\le C_a2^{2a(i-j)}Q_i(x)\quad(j\le i), \qquad \\|H_i(x)\\|\le C_a2^{2ai}.$$
  - Explanation: Corrected heat averages are comparable to ball averages and have monotone inverse matrices with controlled scale growth.
- **C010** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-heat-comparison)): Fix constants $A,C_0\ge1$, where $C_0$ bounds $d(x,y)/r_i$, and fix a bounded region for $x$. There is a fixed exponent $\gamma>0$, which may be taken to be $1/2$, with the following properties for all sufficiently late rescalings. Set $$\label{co:F-definition} \mathcal F_i(x)=2^{-\gamma i}\mathrm{Id}+ \sum_{j=0}^i2^{-\gamma(i-j)}N_j(x).$$ For the prescribed enlargement $A$, $$\label{co:heat-decrement} c_A r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Ar_i}(x)) \le Q_i(x)-Q_{i+1}(x)\le C\mathcal F_i(x).$$ For $d(x,y)\le C_0r_i$ and $v,w\in\mathbb R^n$, $$\label{co:bilinear-heat-comparison} \|v^T(R_i(y)-Q_i(x))w\| \le C\|v\|_{Q_i(x)}\sqrt{\mathcal F_i(x)[w]}.$$ All constants are independent of $i$ and of the expanding cutoff radius. They may depend on the stated fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Dyadic heat decrements control local mass, while a decaying convolution bounds the discrepancy between ball and heat averages.
- **C011** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-inverse-increments)): Fix a testing center $x$, and suppress it in the notation. Let $a>0$ in <a href="#co:slow-QH" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="co:slow-QH">[co:slow-QH]</a> be chosen so that $4a<\gamma$. Set $$B_i=H_{i+1}-H_i,\qquad \Delta_i=Q_i-Q_{i+1}, \qquad a_i=H_i\xi$$ for a fixed $\xi\in\mathbb R^n$. Then, uniformly for every integer $k\ge0$, $$\begin{aligned} &H_i\Delta_iH_i\le B_i\le(1+C\varepsilon_\nu)H_i\Delta_iH_i, \label{co:inverse-increment-order}\\ &\sum_{i<k}\mathcal F_i[a_i]\le C H_k[\xi], \label{co:weighted-total}\\ &C^{-1}H_k\le \mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i<k}H_iN_iH_i\le C H_k. \label{co:central-cumulative} \end{aligned}$$ For every fixed $A\ge1$, the upper sum bound also holds with $r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Ar_i}(x))$ in place of $N_i$.
  - Explanation: Weighted inverse increments and accumulated central mass are comparable without assuming commutation across scales.
- **C012** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-normalization-error)): Fix $C_0\ge1$. For $d(x,y)\le C_0r_i$, put $$A_{i,y}=R_i(y)-Q_i(x),\qquad e_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}\xi-H_i(x)\xi.$$ Then $$\label{co:normalization-error-pointwise} \|e_{i,y}\|_{Q_i(x)}^2 \le C\mathcal F_i(x)[H_i(x)\xi].$$ If at each $i<k$ at most $J$ such centers are chosen, then $$\label{co:normalization-error-sum} \sum_{i<k}\sum_y\|e_{i,y}\|_{Q_i(x)}^2 \le C_J H_k(x)[\xi].$$ For any positive semidefinite matrices $C_{i,y}$ satisfying $C_{i,y}\le\varepsilon_\nu Q_i(x)$, their error cost is $$\label{co:normalization-mass-error} \sum_{i<k}\sum_y C_{i,y}[e_{i,y}] \le C_J\varepsilon_\nu H_k(x)[\xi].$$
  - Explanation: Changing the mean normalization has a quadratic error whose cumulative mass cost is small relative to the final inverse heat form.
- **C013** (bound, [source](#stmt-co-cumulative-comparison)): Fix constants $A_0,C_0,L\ge1$ and a positive integer $J$. At each center $x\in B_{A_0}(p)$ and each scale $i\ge0$, choose at most $J$ centers $y$ with $d(x,y)\le C_0r_i$. The choice may depend arbitrarily on $x,i$. Set $$\label{co:D-definition} C_{i,y}=r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Lr_i}(y)), \qquad D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}C_{i,y}R_i(y)^{-1}.$$ Give the chosen centers weights $\theta_{i,y}\in[0,1]$, and suppose that for every $i$ at least one has weight one and satisfies $B_{Lr_i}(y)\supset B_{r_i}(x)$. Write $$\label{co:ZS-definition} Z_i=\sum_yD_{i,y},\qquad S_i=\sum_y\theta_{i,y}D_{i,y}.$$ There is $C_*<\infty$, depending only on $n,A_0,C_0,L,J$, such that for all sufficiently late rescalings, every such choice, and every integer $k\ge0$, $$\label{co:cumulative-estimates} Z_i\le o(1)H_i(x),\qquad \mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i<k}Z_i\le C_*H_k(x),\qquad H_k(x)\le C_*\left(\mathrm{Id}+\sum_{i<k}S_i\right).$$ The $o(1)$ is uniform over the stated centers, all scales, and all choices satisfying these conditions.
  - Explanation: Bounded-overlap mass forms admit cumulative comparison with inverse heat forms; the reverse comparison requires a weight-one covering center.
- **C014** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-positive-source)): Let $\Omega$ be one of the bounded rescaled domains satisfying <a href="#pi:dirichlet-sobolev" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pi:dirichlet-sobolev">[pi:dirichlet-sobolev]</a>, and suppose that $h\in W^{1,2}_0(\Omega)$ is nonnegative and bounded, with $$\int\langle\nabla h,\nabla\psi\rangle\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m =\int\psi\,\mathrm d\mu$$ for compactly supported Lipschitz tests, where $\mu$ is a finite positive measure. If $\mu(\Omega)\le P$, then $$\int_\Omega\|\nabla h\|\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m\le CP, \qquad \int_\Omega\|\nabla h\|^2\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m\le\\|h\\|_\infty P. \label{pi:positive-source-bounds}$$ The constant in the first inequality depends only on the Sobolev constant, its exponent, and an upper bound for $\mathfrak m(\Omega)$.
  - Explanation: For a bounded nonnegative zero-boundary solution with positive finite source, the gradient integral is controlled linearly by source mass.
- **C015** (construction, [source](#stmt-pi-poisson-pinching)): There are nonnegative numbers $P_i(a)$, defined for every $a\in B_4(p)$ and $i\ge0$, with $$\sup_{a\in B_4(p)}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}P_i(a)=o(1), \label{pi:poisson-sum}$$ and functions $f_i^a$ on $B_{8Sr_i}(a)$ such that, with $q^a=d_a^2/2$, $$\Delta f_i^a=a_i:=n+C_0\kappa r_i^2, \qquad f_i^a-q^a\in W^{1,2}_0(B_{8Sr_i}(a)). \label{pi:poisson-definition}$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on $n,S$ and is the same for every center and every scale. The following bounds hold: $$\begin{aligned} 0\le q^a-f_i^a&\le Cr_i^2, &\sup_{B_{4Sr_i}(a)}\|\nabla f_i^a\|&\le Cr_i, \label{pi:poisson-boundedness}\\ \int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}\|\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)\|^2\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m &\le Cr_i^{n+2}P_i(a), &\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}\|\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)\|\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m &\le Cr_i^{n+1}P_i(a), \label{pi:poisson-errors}\\ \mathcal B_\kappa(f_i^a,1)(B_{2Sr_i}(a)) &\le Cr_i^nP_i(a). \label{pi:poisson-shifted-mass} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Squared-distance Poisson replacements have uniformly summable defects and controlled value, gradient, and shifted Bochner errors.
- **C016** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-weak-cancellation)): Suppose that $f$ has constant Laplacian and $a$ is harmonic on a ball on which both functions have bounded gradients. Put $g_a=\langle\nabla f,\nabla a\rangle$. For interior Sobolev tests $\phi\in W^{1,2}_0$ one has $$\int\langle\nabla g_a,\nabla\phi\rangle\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m =2\int(\operatorname{Hess}f-g)(\nabla a,\nabla\phi)\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m. \label{pi:weak-cancellation-equation}$$ The identity is local, with the right side interpreted on a slightly larger interior ball if a cutoff is needed.
  - Explanation: A constant Laplacian and a harmonic factor yield a weak radial cancellation identity on interior Sobolev tests.
- **C017** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-affine-improvement)): There is an integer $m\ge3$, depending only on $n,S$, such that, with $\theta=2^{-m}$, the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces, all $i$ and all $a\in B_4(p)$. If $w$ is harmonic on $B_{Sr_i}(a)$, then there is an affine combination $F$ of the components of $u$ with $$\\|w-F\\|_{2,B_{S\theta r_i}(a)}^{\mathrm{av}} \le\tfrac12\theta^{3/2} \\|w\\|_{2,B_{Sr_i}(a)}^{\mathrm{av}}. \label{pi:affine-improvement-equation}$$
  - Explanation: Harmonic functions on the stated balls improve toward affine coordinate combinations after a fixed dyadic contraction.
- **C018** (construction, [source](#stmt-pi-radial-invariance)): There are functions $E_i(a)>0$, for $a\in B_4(p)$, such that $$E_i(a)\ge P_i(a),\qquad \sup_a\sum_{i=0}^\infty E_i(a)=o(1),\qquad \inf_a E_i(a)>0\quad\hbox{for each fixed }i. \label{pi:pinching-summability}$$ Let $T=R_i(y)^{-1/2}$ with $d(a,y)\le C_{\mathrm{sep}}r_i$, and put $U=T(u-u(a))/r_i$. In the metric $r_i^{-1}d$, with $\widehat f_i^a=f_i^a/r_i^2$, there is a matrix $A_i^a(T)$ satisfying $\\|A_i^a(T)-\mathrm{Id}\\|=o(1)$ and $$\left\\|\langle\nabla\widehat f_i^a,\nabla U\rangle -A_i^a(T)(U-U(a))\right\\|_{W^{1,2}(B_{S/2}(a))} \le C\sqrt{E_i(a)}. \label{pi:radial-invariance-equation}$$ The ball and all derivatives in this display use the rescaled metric. Arbitrary additive constants may be used in $U$.
  - Explanation: Radial derivatives of normalized coordinates admit affine fits with summable squared defects and positive per-scale floors.
- **C019** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-commutator)): On $B_{8R}(z)$ one has $$\Delta v=0,\qquad \|\nabla v\|\le C,\qquad M(v)(B_{8R}(z))\le Cs^2, \label{pi:pair-poisson-bounds}$$ and $$\\|h-(U(x)-U(z))\\|_{L^2(B_{8R}(z))}\le Cs. \label{pi:constant-direction}$$
  - Explanation: The difference of equal-source Poisson replacements is harmonic, and its gradient pairing with the coordinates identifies the center difference.
- **C020** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-radial-transport)): Work in the rescaled units above. Suppose that $w$ is a bounded Sobolev function on $B_{4R}(z)$ with $\\|w\\|_\infty\le C_1$, and that $$L_zw=w+\ell\cdot U+k+e_w, \qquad \\|e_w\\|_{L^2(B_{4R}(z))}\le C_1s. \label{pi:transport-hypothesis}$$ Put $\alpha=\\|w\\|_{L^2(B_R(z))}$. If $\|\ell\|+\|k\|\le C_1(\alpha+s)$, then $$\\|w\\|_{L^2(B_{2R}(z))}\le C(\alpha+s), \label{pi:transport-conclusion}$$ where $C$ is independent of $\alpha,s$. The constants may depend on $C_1$ and the already fixed radii and coordinate bounds.
  - Explanation: A bounded Sobolev residual satisfying the stated radial equation and affine forcing bound has an outer-ball norm controlled by its inner-ball norm and defect.
- **C021** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-sharp-projection)): For all sufficiently late spaces, there is a vector $b\in\mathbb R^n$ with $\|b\|\le C$ such that $$\\|\nabla(v-b\cdot U)\\|_{L^2(B_{2L+4}(y))}\le Cs. \label{pi:sharp-projection-equation}$$ The constants and the late-space threshold are uniform in the configuration <a href="#pi:center-configuration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pi:center-configuration">[pi:center-configuration]</a> and the dyadic scale.
  - Explanation: The specific difference of Poisson replacements in the fixed two-center configuration admits a sharp gradient approximation by the harmonic coordinate span.
- **C022** (bound, [source](#stmt-pi-directional-pinching)): For every fixed $L,C_{\mathrm{sep}}>1$, the functions $E_i$ may be chosen as in <a href="#pi:definition-E" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pi:definition-E">[pi:definition-E]</a>, with the uniform summability and positive floors in <a href="#pi:pinching-summability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pi:pinching-summability">[pi:pinching-summability]</a>, so that the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces. Define $$D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1} \bigl[r_i^{2-n}M(u)(B_{Lr_i}(y))\bigr]R_i(y)^{-1}.$$ For every configuration <a href="#pi:center-configuration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pi:center-configuration">[pi:center-configuration]</a>, $$D_{i,y}[u(x)-u(z)] \le Cr_i^2\bigl(E_i(x)+E_i(z)\bigr). \label{pi:directional-estimate}$$ The same functions work simultaneously for all such centers and all dyadic scales. All enlargement constants are fixed before choosing the late-space threshold.
  - Explanation: Bochner mass evaluated on actual coordinate differences is bounded by a uniformly summable directional defect across all dyadic scales.
- **C023** (bound, [source](#stmt-re-field-estimates)): At $P=P_x$, $x\in B_1(p)$, write $r=r_i$, $H=H_i(x)$, $Q=Q_i(x)$, $Z=Z_i(x)$, $S=S_i(x)$, and $E=E_i(x)$. There is a linear error $e(\xi)$ such that $$\begin{aligned} r^{-1}\|v_i\|_Q&\le C\sqrt\varepsilon\sqrt E, \label{re:field-value}\\ (\mathrm Dv_i)\xi&=S\xi+e(\xi), \label{re:field-linear}\\ \|S\xi\|_Q&\le\sqrt\varepsilon\sqrt{Z[\xi]}, &\|e(\xi)\|_Q&\le C\sqrt\varepsilon\sqrt E\,\|\xi\|_H, \label{re:field-small-linear}\\ \|\xi^Te(\xi)\|&\le C\sqrt{Z[\xi]E}\,\|\xi\|_H, \label{re:field-diagonal}\\ r\|\mathrm D^2v_i(\xi,\xi)\|_Q &\le C\left(\sqrt{Z[\xi]}\,\|\xi\|_H +\sqrt E\,\|\xi\|_H^2\right). \label{re:field-second} \end{aligned}$$ There is a fixed $C_v\ge1$ such that, in every order $m\ge0$, $$\label{re:field-jets} r_i^{m-1}\\|\mathrm D^m v_i(P_x)\\|_{H_i(x)\to Q_i(x)} \le\varepsilon C_v^{m+1}(m!)^s.$$
  - Explanation: Localized smooth covector fields have directional value and derivative bounds, alongside uniform all-order Gevrey estimates.
- **C024** (bound, [source](#stmt-re-inverse-majorant)): Fix $\sigma\ge1$ and put $L_0=1$ and $$L_m=(m+1)((m+1)!)^\sigma\qquad(m\ge1).$$ Then $$\label{re:convolution-decay} \delta_m:=\sum_{l=1}^{m-1}\frac{L_lL_{m-l}}{L_m} \longrightarrow0, \qquad \sum_{l=0}^m L_lL_{m-l}\le C_\sigma L_m.$$ Suppose a smooth matrix-valued function $J$ is invertible at a point and, in Euclidean operator norms at that point, $$\\|J^{-1}\\|\le\sqrt A,\qquad \frac{\\|\mathrm D^mJ\\|}{m!}\le B^mL_m\quad(m\ge1), \qquad A,B\ge1.$$ There is a constant $C_{A,\sigma}$, independent of $m$ and $B$, such that $$\label{re:inverse-jets} \frac{\\|\mathrm D^m(J^{-1})\\|}{m!} \le C_{A,\sigma}B^mL_m\qquad(m\ge0).$$ The assertion also holds for the inverse transpose.
  - Explanation: Factorial convolution majorants allow inversion to preserve the derivative growth base under the stated pointwise inverse and derivative bounds.
- **C025** (bound, [source](#stmt-re-jet-step)): Choose $B\ge C_v$ sufficiently large and then $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small, depending on $A,\sigma,B$ and the fixed data. If <a href="#re:metric-bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:metric-bootstrap">[re:metric-bootstrap]</a> and <a href="#re:jet-bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:jet-bootstrap">[re:jet-bootstrap]</a> hold at level $i$, the update <a href="#re:iteration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:iteration">[re:iteration]</a> satisfies <a href="#re:jet-bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:jet-bootstrap">[re:jet-bootstrap]</a> at level $i+1$. This assertion does not require the new-level metric bound.
  - Explanation: With constants chosen in the stated order, an update preserves every higher-derivative bound using old-level metric and jet bounds.
- **C026** (bound, [source](#stmt-re-metric-iteration)): There are fixed $A,B$ and a sufficiently small flatness threshold such that the maps <a href="#re:iteration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:iteration">[re:iteration]</a> are smooth on all of $\Omega$ for every finite $i$, their Jacobians are invertible there, and <a href="#re:metric-bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:metric-bootstrap">[re:metric-bootstrap]</a>–<a href="#re:jet-bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="re:jet-bootstrap">[re:jet-bootstrap]</a> hold for all $x\in B_1(p)$ and every $i$.
  - Explanation: All finite iterates are smooth on the common value domain with invertible Jacobians and simultaneous metric and jet bounds.
- **C027** (construction, [source](#stmt-re-limit-chart)): For sufficiently flat data in the preceding construction, there is $\rho>0$ such that the uniform limit $$F(x)=\lim_{i\to\infty}h_i(u(x)),\qquad x\in B_1(p),$$ restricts to a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism from $B_\rho(p)$ onto an open subset of $\mathbb R^n$. Its distance comparisons use the ambient distance $d$ restricted to $B_\rho(p)$.
  - Explanation: For sufficiently flat input, the uniform limit is an open bi-Lipschitz chart on a smaller ambient ball.

        "exact_quote": "For every integer $n\\ge2$ there is a constant $L_n\\ge1$ with the following property. The measure $$\\label{co:M-definition}\n M(w)=\\frac12\\Delta\\Gamma(w)+\\kappa\\Gamma(w)\\mathfrak m$$ is locally finite and nonnegative. For every constant $b$, $$\\label{co:shifted-bochner}\n M(w)-(2b\\Delta w-nb^2)\\mathfrak m\n \\ge |\\operatorname{Hess}w-bg|^2\\mathfrak m.$$ On the real vector space of local constant-Laplacian functions, the left side is a nonnegative quadratic form in the pair $(w,b)$, with both entries scaled together. All these statements hold on interior balls for weak solutions with locally bounded $L^2$ norm, after using the interior estimates to obtain local boundedness.*\n\n\n*On fixed interior scales, bounded Lipschitz cutoffs with bounded Laplacian are available. Under uniform local volume comparison and a uniform rescaled curvature bound, their bounds, local Poincare and Sobolev constants, and interior harmonic and Poisson estimates are uniform. In particular, on a fixed-radius ball in the rescaled spaces below there is a number $d_*>1$ and a constant $C$ such that $$\\label{co:dirichlet-sobolev}\n \\|h\\|_{L^2}+\\|h\\|_{L^{2d_*}}\n \\le C\\|\\nabla h\\|_{L^2},\\qquad h\\in W^{1,2}_0(B),$$ whenever the ball and a fixed larger ball are contained in the working region.",
      "explanation": "Constant-Laplacian solutions admit local test calculus, a positive shifted Bochner measure, and uniform interior Dirichlet estimates.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-local-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(Y,d,\\mathfrak m)$ be a noncollapsed $\\mathrm{RCD}(-\\kappa,n)$ space and let $\\Omega\\subset Y$ be open. A locally bounded solution of $\\Delta w=a$ in $\\Omega$, with $a$ constant, is locally Lipschitz and is a local test function. In particular it has a local $L^2$ Hessian, $\\Gamma(w_1,w_2)\\in W^{1,2}_{\\rm loc}(\\Omega)$ for two such functions, and the Hessian product rule holds locally. The measure $$\\label{co:M-definition}\n M(w)=\\frac12\\Delta\\Gamma(w)+\\kappa\\Gamma(w)\\mathfrak m$$ is locally finite and nonnegative. For every constant $b$, $$\\label{co:shifted-bochner}\n M(w)-(2b\\Delta w-nb^2)\\mathfrak m\n \\ge |\\operatorname{Hess}w-bg|^2\\mathfrak m.$$ On the real vector space of local constant-Laplacian functions, the left side is a nonnegative quadratic form in the pair $(w,b)$, with both entries scaled together. All these statements hold on interior balls for weak solutions with locally bounded $L^2$ norm, after using the interior estimates to obtain local boundedness.*\n\n\n*On fixed interior scales, bounded Lipschitz cutoffs with bounded Laplacian are available. Under uniform local volume comparison and a uniform rescaled curvature bound, their bounds, local Poincare and Sobolev constants, and interior harmonic and Poisson estimates are uniform. In particular, on a fixed-radius ball in the rescaled spaces below there is a number $d_*>1$ and a constant $C$ such that $$\\label{co:dirichlet-sobolev}\n \\|h\\|_{L^2}+\\|h\\|_{L^{2d_*}}\n \\le C\\|\\nabla h\\|_{L^2},\\qquad h\\in W^{1,2}_0(B),$$ whenever the ball and a fixed larger ball are contained in the working region.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-flat-setup",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an $n$-regular point $p$ in the space of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. There are rescalings $$(X_\\nu,d_\\nu,\\mathfrak m_\\nu,p_\\nu)\n =(X,s_\\nu^{-1}d,s_\\nu^{-n}\\mathcal H_d^n,p),\n \\qquad s_\\nu\\downarrow0,$$ numbers $\\kappa_\\nu\\downarrow0$, and radii $T_\\nu\\uparrow\\infty$ with $\\kappa_\\nu T_\\nu^2\\to0$, having the following properties. The space $X_\\nu$ is $\\mathrm{RCD}(-\\kappa_\\nu,n)$. For a sequence $\\delta_\\nu\\downarrow0$, all balls with center in $B_{64T_\\nu}(p_\\nu)$ and radius at most $64T_\\nu$ satisfy $$\\label{co:uniform-flatness}\n \\left|\\frac{\\mathfrak m_\\nu(B_r(y))}{\\omega_n r^n}-1\\right|\n \\le\\delta_\\nu,\n \\qquad\n d_{GH}\\bigl(\\overline B_r(y),\\overline B_r(0^n)\\bigr)\n \\le\\delta_\\nu r.$$ There are harmonic columns $u_\\nu$ on $B_{32T_\\nu}(p_\\nu)$, converging on every fixed ball locally uniformly and strongly in $W^{1,2}$ to Euclidean coordinates, and $$\\label{co:original-gradient-bound}\n |\\nabla u_\\nu|\\le C_n\n \\quad\\text{on }B_{16T_\\nu}(p_\\nu).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-co-harmonic-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "For every pair of fixed constants $A,C\\ge1$, the matrices $R_i(y)$ are positive definite for $y\\in B_A(p)$ and $i\\ge0$. In the rescaled ball $B_{Cr_i}(y)$, the map $$\\label{co:normalized-column}\n U_{i,y}=\\frac{R_i(y)^{-1/2}(u-u(y))}{r_i}$$ is, after an orthogonal change of target coordinates, an $o(1)$ approximation to Euclidean coordinates, uniformly and strongly in interior $W^{1,2}$. Its gradients are uniformly bounded on each fixed interior enlargement. Its Gram matrix converges strongly in every finite $L^p$ on such enlargements to $\\mathrm{Id}$.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every fixed $C\\ge1$, if $d(y,z)\\le Cr_i$ and $C^{-1}r_i\\le r_j\\le Cr_i$, then $$\\label{co:nearby-R}\n (1-o(1))R_i(y)\\le R_j(z)\\le(1+o(1))R_i(y).$$ For every $a>0$, after choosing a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\\label{co:slow-R}\n C_a^{-1}2^{-2a|i-j|}R_j(y)\n \\le R_i(y)\\le C_a2^{2a|i-j|}R_j(y).$$ The constants are uniform in the center and the two indices. In particular, since $R_0(y)=\\mathrm{Id}+o(1)$ on fixed balls, $$\\label{co:R-lower}\n c_a r_i^{2a}\\mathrm{Id}\\le R_i(y)\\le C\\mathrm{Id}.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },

Its gradients are uniformly bounded on each fixed interior enlargement.         "anchor": "stmt-co-open-coordinates",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $A\\ge1$, the map $u$ is a homeomorphism from $B_A(p)$ onto an open subset of $\\mathbb R^n$, once the rescaling is sufficiently late. There is a fixed $c>0$ such that, whenever the balls involved stay in a fixed interior working region, $$\\label{co:ellipsoid-containment}\n \\{P:|P-u(x)|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}<c r_i\\}\n \\subset u(B_{r_i/2}(x)).$$ Moreover, for every fixed $D\\ge1$, if $D^{-1}r_i\\le d(x,z)\\le Dr_i$, then $$\\label{co:separation-scale}\n \\bigl||u(z)-u(x)|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}-d(x,z)\\bigr|\n \\le o_D(1)r_i.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-co-open-coordinates",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-kernel-measures",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $x,y$ in a fixed testing region, and $d(x,y)\\le C_0r$, where $0<r\\le1$. Put $$K_t(z)=\\mathop{\\mathchoice\n {\\displaystyle\\int\\mkern-18mu-}\n {\\textstyle\\int\\mkern-15mu-}\n {\\scriptstyle\\int\\mkern-12mu-}\n {\\scriptscriptstyle\\int\\mkern-10mu-}}\\nolimits_{B_r(y)}p_t(a,z)\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m(a),\n \\qquad D=d(x,z).$$ After enlarging fixed constants, the following bounds hold at every source pole $z$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_t(z)&\\le C r^{-n}e^{-cD^2/r^2},&&0<t\\le r^2,\n       \\label{co:short-kernel}\\\\\n |K_t(z)-p_t(x,z)|&\\le C r t^{-(n+1)/2}e^{-cD^2/t},\n       &&r^2\\le t\\le1.\\label{co:long-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $f\\in W^{1,2}\\cap L^\\infty$ has compact support and its Laplacian is a finite signed measure $\\mu$ of compact support, then $$\\label{co:semigroup-measure}\n P_t f-P_s f=\\int_s^t P_\\tau\\mu\\,\\mathrm d\\tau,\n \\qquad 0<s<t,$$ using the continuous representatives. In the application $f=\\chi\\Gamma(u_j,u_k)$, this identity can also be averaged over a ball and passed to $s\\downarrow0$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-kernel-measures",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $x,y$ in a fixed testing region, and $d(x,y)\\le C_0r$, where $0<r\\le1$. Put $$K_t(z)=\\mathop{\\mathchoice\n {\\displaystyle\\int\\mkern-18mu-}\n {\\textstyle\\int\\mkern-15mu-}\n {\\scriptstyle\\int\\mkern-12mu-}\n {\\scriptscriptstyle\\int\\mkern-10mu-}}\\nolimits_{B_r(y)}p_t(a,z)\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m(a),\n \\qquad D=d(x,z).$$ After enlarging fixed constants, the following bounds hold at every source pole $z$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_t(z)&\\le C r^{-n}e^{-cD^2/r^2},&&0<t\\le r^2,\n       \\label{co:short-kernel}\\\\\n |K_t(z)-p_t(x,z)|&\\le C r t^{-(n+1)/2}e^{-cD^2/t},\n       &&r^2\\le t\\le1.\\label{co:long-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $f\\in W^{1,2}\\cap L^\\infty$ has compact support and its Laplacian is a finite signed measure $\\mu$ of compact support, then $$\\label{co:semigroup-measure}\n P_t f-P_s f=\\int_s^t P_\\tau\\mu\\,\\mathrm d\\tau,\n \\qquad 0<s<t,$$ using the continuous representatives. In the application $f=\\chi\\Gamma(u_j,u_k)$, this identity can also be averaged over a ball and passed to $s\\downarrow0$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$, $x,y$ in a fixed testing region, and $d(x,y)\\le C_0r$, where $0<r\\le1$. Put $$K_t(z)=\\mathop{\\mathchoice\n {\\displaystyle\\int\\mkern-18mu-}\n {\\textstyle\\int\\mkern-15mu-}\n {\\scriptstyle\\int\\mkern-12mu-}\n {\\scriptscriptstyle\\int\\mkern-10mu-}}\\nolimits_{B_r(y)}p_t(a,z)\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m(a),\n \\qquad D=d(x,z).$$ After enlarging fixed constants, the following bounds hold at every source pole $z$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_t(z)&\\le C r^{-n}e^{-cD^2/r^2},&&0<t\\le r^2,\n       \\label{co:short-kernel}\\\\\n |K_t(z)-p_t(x,z)|&\\le C r t^{-(n+1)/2}e^{-cD^2/t},\n       &&r^2\\le t\\le1.\\label{co:long-kernel}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $f\\in W^{1,2}\\cap L^\\infty$ has compact support and its Laplacian is a finite signed measure $\\mu$ of compact support, then $$\\label{co:semigroup-measure}\n P_t f-P_s f=\\int_s^t P_\\tau\\mu\\,\\mathrm d\\tau,\n \\qquad 0<s<t,$$ using the continuous representatives. In the application $f=\\chi\\Gamma(u_j,u_k)$, this identity can also be averaged over a ball and passed to $s\\downarrow0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-heat-matrices",
        "exact_quote": "There is $\\lambda=\\lambda_\\nu>0$, with $\\lambda_\\nu\\to0$, such that, at all fixed testing centers and $0<t\\le1$, $$\\label{co:heat-derivative}\n P_t(\\chi M)\n \\le\\partial_t\\bigl(P_t(\\chi G)+\\lambda t\\mathrm{Id}\\bigr)\n \\le 3P_t(\\chi M)+C\\mathrm{Id}.$$ Define $$\\label{co:QH-definition}\n Q_i(x)=P_{r_i^2}(\\chi G)(x)+\\lambda r_i^2\\mathrm{Id},\n \\qquad H_i(x)=Q_i(x)^{-1}.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n &(1-o(1))R_i(x)\\le Q_i(x)\\le(1+o(1))R_i(x),\n       \\qquad Q_0(x)=\\mathrm{Id}+o(1),\\label{co:QR-comparison}\\\\\n &H_i(x)\\le H_{i+1}(x)\\le(1+o(1))H_i(x).\n       \\label{co:H-monotone}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The nearby-center and slow-growth estimates of Lemma <a href=\"#co:harmonic-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:harmonic-normalization\">5</a> hold for $Q_i$ as well. In particular, for every prescribed $a>0$, with a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\\label{co:slow-QH}\n Q_j(x)\\le C_a2^{2a(i-j)}Q_i(x)\\quad(j\\le i),\n \\qquad \\|H_i(x)\\|\\le C_a2^{2ai}.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-heat-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix constants $A,C_0\\ge1$, where $C_0$ bounds $d(x,y)/r_i$, and fix a bounded region for $x$. There is a fixed exponent $\\gamma>0$, which may be taken to be $1/2$, with the following properties for all sufficiently late rescalings. Set $$\\label{co:F-definition}\n \\mathcal F_i(x)=2^{-\\gamma i}\\mathrm{Id}+\n       \\sum_{j=0}^i2^{-\\gamma(i-j)}N_j(x).$$ For the prescribed enlargement $A$, $$\\label{co:heat-decrement}\n c_A r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Ar_i}(x))\n \\le Q_i(x)-Q_{i+1}(x)\\le C\\mathcal F_i(x).$$ For $d(x,y)\\le C_0r_i$ and $v,w\\in\\mathbb R^n$, $$\\label{co:bilinear-heat-comparison}\n |v^T(R_i(y)-Q_i(x))w|\n \\le C|v|_{Q_i(x)}\\sqrt{\\mathcal F_i(x)[w]}.$$ All constants are independent of $i$ and of the expanding cutoff radius. They may depend on the stated fixed parameters.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-heat-comparison",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-inverse-increments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a testing center $x$, and suppress it in the notation. Let $a>0$ in <a href=\"#co:slow-QH\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"co:slow-QH\">[co:slow-QH]</a> be chosen so that $4a<\\gamma$. Set $$B_i=H_{i+1}-H_i,\\qquad \\Delta_i=Q_i-Q_{i+1},\n \\qquad a_i=H_i\\xi$$ for a fixed $\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^n$. Then, uniformly for every integer $k\\ge0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &H_i\\Delta_iH_i\\le B_i\\le(1+C\\varepsilon_\\nu)H_i\\Delta_iH_i,\n             \\label{co:inverse-increment-order}\\\\\n &\\sum_{i<k}\\mathcal F_i[a_i]\\le C H_k[\\xi],\n             \\label{co:weighted-total}\\\\\n &C^{-1}H_k\\le \\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}H_iN_iH_i\\le C H_k.\n             \\label{co:central-cumulative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every fixed $A\\ge1$, the upper sum bound also holds with $r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Ar_i}(x))$ in place of $N_i$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-inverse-increments",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a testing center $x$, and suppress it in the notation.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-normalization-error",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$. For $d(x,y)\\le C_0r_i$, put $$A_{i,y}=R_i(y)-Q_i(x),\\qquad\n e_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}\\xi-H_i(x)\\xi.$$ Then $$\\label{co:normalization-error-pointwise}\n |e_{i,y}|_{Q_i(x)}^2\n \\le C\\mathcal F_i(x)[H_i(x)\\xi].$$ If at each $i<k$ at most $J$ such centers are chosen, then $$\\label{co:normalization-error-sum}\n \\sum_{i<k}\\sum_y|e_{i,y}|_{Q_i(x)}^2\n \\le C_J H_k(x)[\\xi].$$ For any positive semidefinite matrices $C_{i,y}$ satisfying $C_{i,y}\\le\\varepsilon_\\nu Q_i(x)$, their error cost is $$\\label{co:normalization-mass-error}\n \\sum_{i<k}\\sum_y C_{i,y}[e_{i,y}]\n \\le C_J\\varepsilon_\\nu H_k(x)[\\xi].$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-normalization-error",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"

          "anchor": "stmt-co-normalization-error",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0\\ge1$. For $d(x,y)\\le C_0r_i$, put $$A_{i,y}=R_i(y)-Q_i(x),\\qquad\n e_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}\\xi-H_i(x)\\xi.$$ Then $$\\label{co:normalization-error-pointwise}\n |e_{i,y}|_{Q_i(x)}^2\n \\le C\\mathcal F_i(x)[H_i(x)\\xi].$$ If at each $i<k$ at most $J$ such centers are chosen, then $$\\label{co:normalization-error-sum}\n \\sum_{i<k}\\sum_y|e_{i,y}|_{Q_i(x)}^2\n \\le C_J H_k(x)[\\xi].$$ For any positive semidefinite matrices $C_{i,y}$ satisfying $C_{i,y}\\le\\varepsilon_\\nu Q_i(x)$, their error cost is $$\\label{co:normalization-mass-error}\n \\sum_{i<k}\\sum_y C_{i,y}[e_{i,y}]\n \\le C_J\\varepsilon_\\nu H_k(x)[\\xi].$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-co-cumulative-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix constants $A_0,C_0,L\\ge1$ and a positive integer $J$. At each center $x\\in B_{A_0}(p)$ and each scale $i\\ge0$, choose at most $J$ centers $y$ with $d(x,y)\\le C_0r_i$. The choice may depend arbitrarily on $x,i$. Set $$\\label{co:D-definition}\n C_{i,y}=r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Lr_i}(y)),\n \\qquad D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}C_{i,y}R_i(y)^{-1}.$$ Give the chosen centers weights $\\theta_{i,y}\\in[0,1]$, and suppose that for every $i$ at least one has weight one and satisfies $B_{Lr_i}(y)\\supset B_{r_i}(x)$. Write $$\\label{co:ZS-definition}\n Z_i=\\sum_yD_{i,y},\\qquad\n S_i=\\sum_y\\theta_{i,y}D_{i,y}.$$ There is $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $n,A_0,C_0,L,J$, such that for all sufficiently late rescalings, every such choice, and every integer $k\\ge0$, $$\\label{co:cumulative-estimates}\n Z_i\\le o(1)H_i(x),\\qquad\n \\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}Z_i\\le C_*H_k(x),\\qquad\n H_k(x)\\le C_*\\left(\\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}S_i\\right).$$ The $o(1)$ is uniform over the stated centers, all scales, and all choices satisfying these conditions.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

Write $$\\label{co:ZS-definition}\n Z_i=\\sum_yD_{i,y},\\qquad\n S_i=\\sum_y\\theta_{i,y}D_{i,y}.$$ There is $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $n,A_0,C_0,L,J$, such that for all sufficiently late rescalings, every such choice, and every integer $k\\ge0$, $$\\label{co:cumulative-estimates}\n Z_i\\le o(1)H_i(x),\\qquad\n \\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}Z_i\\le C_*H_k(x),\\qquad\n H_k(x)\\le C_*\\left(\\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}S_i\\right).$$ The $o(1)$ is uniform over the stated centers, all scales, and all choices satisfying these conditions.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix constants $A_0,C_0,L\\ge1$ and a positive integer $J$.       "explanation": "Bounded-overlap mass forms admit cumulative comparison with inverse heat forms; the reverse comparison requires a weight-one covering center.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pi-positive-source",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be one of the bounded rescaled domains satisfying <a href=\"#pi:dirichlet-sobolev\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:dirichlet-sobolev\">[pi:dirichlet-sobolev]</a>, and suppose that $h\\in W^{1,2}_0(\\Omega)$ is nonnegative and bounded, with $$\\int\\langle\\nabla h,\\nabla\\psi\\rangle\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n =\\int\\psi\\,\\mathrm d\\mu$$ for compactly supported Lipschitz tests, where $\\mu$ is a finite positive measure. If $\\mu(\\Omega)\\le P$, then $$\\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le CP,\n \\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le\\|h\\|_\\infty P.\n \\label{pi:positive-source-bounds}$$ The constant in the first inequality depends only on the Sobolev constant, its exponent, and an upper bound for $\\mathfrak m(\\Omega)$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pi-positive-source",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be one of the bounded rescaled domains satisfying <a href=\"#pi:dirichlet-sobolev\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:dirichlet-sobolev\">[pi:dirichlet-sobolev]</a>, and suppose that $h\\in W^{1,2}_0(\\Omega)$ is nonnegative and bounded, with $$\\int\\langle\\nabla h,\\nabla\\psi\\rangle\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n =\\int\\psi\\,\\mathrm d\\mu$$ for compactly supported Lipschitz tests, where $\\mu$ is a finite positive measure. If $\\mu(\\Omega)\\le P$, then $$\\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le CP,\n \\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le\\|h\\|_\\infty P.\n \\label{pi:positive-source-bounds}$$ The constant in the first inequality depends only on the Sobolev constant, its exponent, and an upper bound for $\\mathfrak m(\\Omega)$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Omega$ be one of the bounded rescaled domains satisfying <a href=\"#pi:dirichlet-sobolev\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:dirichlet-sobolev\">[pi:dirichlet-sobolev]</a>, and suppose that $h\\in W^{1,2}_0(\\Omega)$ is nonnegative and bounded, with $$\\int\\langle\\nabla h,\\nabla\\psi\\rangle\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n =\\int\\psi\\,\\mathrm d\\mu$$ for compactly supported Lipschitz tests, where $\\mu$ is a finite positive measure. If $\\mu(\\Omega)\\le P$, then $$\\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le CP,\n \\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le\\|h\\|_\\infty P.\n \\label{pi:positive-source-bounds}$$ The constant in the first inequality depends only on the Sobolev constant, its exponent, and an upper bound for $\\mathfrak m(\\Omega)$.",
      "explanation": "For a bounded nonnegative zero-boundary solution with positive finite source, the gradient integral is controlled linearly by source mass.",

If $\\mu(\\Omega)\\le P$, then $$\\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le CP,\n \\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega|\\nabla h|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\\le\\|h\\|_\\infty P.\n \\label{pi:positive-source-bounds}$$ The constant in the first inequality depends only on the Sobolev constant, its exponent, and an upper bound for $\\mathfrak m(\\Omega)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pi-poisson-pinching",
        "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative numbers $P_i(a)$, defined for every $a\\in B_4(p)$ and $i\\ge0$, with $$\\sup_{a\\in B_4(p)}\\sum_{i=0}^{\\infty}P_i(a)=o(1),\n \\label{pi:poisson-sum}$$ and functions $f_i^a$ on $B_{8Sr_i}(a)$ such that, with $q^a=d_a^2/2$, $$\\Delta f_i^a=a_i:=n+C_0\\kappa r_i^2,\n \\qquad f_i^a-q^a\\in W^{1,2}_0(B_{8Sr_i}(a)).\n \\label{pi:poisson-definition}$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on $n,S$ and is the same for every center and every scale. The following bounds hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le q^a-f_i^a&\\le Cr_i^2,\n &\\sup_{B_{4Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla f_i^a|&\\le Cr_i,\n \\label{pi:poisson-boundedness}\\\\\n \\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+2}P_i(a),\n &\\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+1}P_i(a),\n \\label{pi:poisson-errors}\\\\\n \\mathcal B_\\kappa(f_i^a,1)(B_{2Sr_i}(a))\n &\\le Cr_i^nP_i(a).\n \\label{pi:poisson-shifted-mass}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pi-poisson-pinching",
          "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative numbers $P_i(a)$, defined for every $a\\in B_4(p)$ and $i\\ge0$, with $$\\sup_{a\\in B_4(p)}\\sum_{i=0}^{\\infty}P_i(a)=o(1),\n \\label{pi:poisson-sum}$$ and functions $f_i^a$ on $B_{8Sr_i}(a)$ such that, with $q^a=d_a^2/2$, $$\\Delta f_i^a=a_i:=n+C_0\\kappa r_i^2,\n \\qquad f_i^a-q^a\\in W^{1,2}_0(B_{8Sr_i}(a)).\n \\label{pi:poisson-definition}$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on $n,S$ and is the same for every center and every scale. The following bounds hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le q^a-f_i^a&\\le Cr_i^2,\n &\\sup_{B_{4Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla f_i^a|&\\le Cr_i,\n \\label{pi:poisson-boundedness}\\\\\n \\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+2}P_i(a),\n &\\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+1}P_i(a),\n \\label{pi:poisson-errors}\\\\\n \\mathcal B_\\kappa(f_i^a,1)(B_{2Sr_i}(a))\n &\\le Cr_i^nP_i(a).\n \\label{pi:poisson-shifted-mass}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"

      "exact_statement": "There are nonnegative numbers $P_i(a)$, defined for every $a\\in B_4(p)$ and $i\\ge0$, with $$\\sup_{a\\in B_4(p)}\\sum_{i=0}^{\\infty}P_i(a)=o(1),\n \\label{pi:poisson-sum}$$ and functions $f_i^a$ on $B_{8Sr_i}(a)$ such that, with $q^a=d_a^2/2$, $$\\Delta f_i^a=a_i:=n+C_0\\kappa r_i^2,\n \\qquad f_i^a-q^a\\in W^{1,2}_0(B_{8Sr_i}(a)).\n \\label{pi:poisson-definition}$$ Here $C_0$ depends only on $n,S$ and is the same for every center and every scale.       "explanation": "Squared-distance Poisson replacements have uniformly summable defects and controlled value, gradient, and shifted Bochner errors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pi-weak-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $f$ has constant Laplacian and $a$ is harmonic on a ball on which both functions have bounded gradients. Put $g_a=\\langle\\nabla f,\\nabla a\\rangle$. For interior Sobolev tests $\\phi\\in W^{1,2}_0$ one has $$\\int\\langle\\nabla g_a,\\nabla\\phi\\rangle\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n =2\\int(\\operatorname{Hess}f-g)(\\nabla a,\\nabla\\phi)\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m.\n \\label{pi:weak-cancellation-equation}$$ The identity is local, with the right side interpreted on a slightly larger interior ball if a cutoff is needed.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pi-weak-cancellation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $f$ has constant Laplacian and $a$ is harmonic on a ball on which both functions have bounded gradients. Put $g_a=\\langle\\nabla f,\\nabla a\\rangle$. For interior Sobolev tests $\\phi\\in W^{1,2}_0$ one has $$\\int\\langle\\nabla g_a,\\nabla\\phi\\rangle\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n =2\\int(\\operatorname{Hess}f-g)(\\nabla a,\\nabla\\phi)\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m.\n \\label{pi:weak-cancellation-equation}$$ The identity is local, with the right side interpreted on a slightly larger interior ball if a cutoff is needed.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $f$ has constant Laplacian and $a$ is harmonic on a ball on which both functions have bounded gradients. Put $g_a=\\langle\\nabla f,\\nabla a\\rangle$.       "explanation": "A constant Laplacian and a harmonic factor yield a weak radial cancellation identity on interior Sobolev tests.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pi-affine-improvement",
        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $m\\ge3$, depending only on $n,S$, such that, with $\\theta=2^{-m}$, the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces, all $i$ and all $a\\in B_4(p)$. If $w$ is harmonic on $B_{Sr_i}(a)$, then there is an affine combination $F$ of the components of $u$ with $$\\|w-F\\|_{2,B_{S\\theta r_i}(a)}^{\\mathrm{av}}\n \\le\\tfrac12\\theta^{3/2}\n       \\|w\\|_{2,B_{Sr_i}(a)}^{\\mathrm{av}}.\n \\label{pi:affine-improvement-equation}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pi-affine-improvement",
          "exact_quote": "There is an integer $m\\ge3$, depending only on $n,S$, such that, with $\\theta=2^{-m}$, the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces, all $i$ and all $a\\in B_4(p)$.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an integer $m\\ge3$, depending only on $n,S$, such that, with $\\theta=2^{-m}$, the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces, all $i$ and all $a\\in B_4(p)$.         "exact_quote": "There are functions $E_i(a)>0$, for $a\\in B_4(p)$, such that $$E_i(a)\\ge P_i(a),\\qquad\n \\sup_a\\sum_{i=0}^\\infty E_i(a)=o(1),\\qquad\n \\inf_a E_i(a)>0\\quad\\hbox{for each fixed }i.\n \\label{pi:pinching-summability}$$ Let $T=R_i(y)^{-1/2}$ with $d(a,y)\\le C_{\\mathrm{sep}}r_i$, and put $U=T(u-u(a))/r_i$. In the metric $r_i^{-1}d$, with $\\widehat f_i^a=f_i^a/r_i^2$, there is a matrix $A_i^a(T)$ satisfying $\\|A_i^a(T)-\\mathrm{Id}\\|=o(1)$ and $$\\left\\|\\langle\\nabla\\widehat f_i^a,\\nabla U\\rangle\n       -A_i^a(T)(U-U(a))\\right\\|_{W^{1,2}(B_{S/2}(a))}\n \\le C\\sqrt{E_i(a)}.\n \\label{pi:radial-invariance-equation}$$ The ball and all derivatives in this display use the rescaled metric.         "anchor": "stmt-pi-radial-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Work in the rescaled units above.         "anchor": "stmt-pi-directional-pinching",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $L,C_{\\mathrm{sep}}>1$, the functions $E_i$ may be chosen as in <a href=\"#pi:definition-E\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:definition-E\">[pi:definition-E]</a>, with the uniform summability and positive floors in <a href=\"#pi:pinching-summability\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:pinching-summability\">[pi:pinching-summability]</a>, so that the following holds for all sufficiently late spaces. Define $$D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}\n       \\bigl[r_i^{2-n}M(u)(B_{Lr_i}(y))\\bigr]R_i(y)^{-1}.$$ For every configuration <a href=\"#pi:center-configuration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pi:center-configuration\">[pi:center-configuration]</a>, $$D_{i,y}[u(x)-u(z)]\n \\le Cr_i^2\\bigl(E_i(x)+E_i(z)\\bigr).\n \\label{pi:directional-estimate}$$ The same functions work simultaneously for all such centers and all dyadic scales.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-re-field-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "At $P=P_x$, $x\\in B_1(p)$, write $r=r_i$, $H=H_i(x)$, $Q=Q_i(x)$, $Z=Z_i(x)$, $S=S_i(x)$, and $E=E_i(x)$. There is a linear error $e(\\xi)$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n r^{-1}|v_i|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E,\n       \\label{re:field-value}\\\\\n (\\mathrm Dv_i)\\xi&=S\\xi+e(\\xi),\n       \\label{re:field-linear}\\\\\n |S\\xi|_Q&\\le\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]},\n       &|e(\\xi)|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-small-linear}\\\\\n |\\xi^Te(\\xi)|&\\le C\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]E}\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-diagonal}\\\\\n r|\\mathrm D^2v_i(\\xi,\\xi)|_Q\n       &\\le C\\left(\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]}\\,|\\xi|_H\n                          +\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H^2\\right).\n       \\label{re:field-second}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a fixed $C_v\\ge1$ such that, in every order $m\\ge0$, $$\\label{re:field-jets}\n r_i^{m-1}\\|\\mathrm D^m v_i(P_x)\\|_{H_i(x)\\to Q_i(x)}\n       \\le\\varepsilon C_v^{m+1}(m!)^s.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-re-field-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "At $P=P_x$, $x\\in B_1(p)$, write $r=r_i$, $H=H_i(x)$, $Q=Q_i(x)$, $Z=Z_i(x)$, $S=S_i(x)$, and $E=E_i(x)$. There is a linear error $e(\\xi)$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n r^{-1}|v_i|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E,\n       \\label{re:field-value}\\\\\n (\\mathrm Dv_i)\\xi&=S\\xi+e(\\xi),\n       \\label{re:field-linear}\\\\\n |S\\xi|_Q&\\le\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]},\n       &|e(\\xi)|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-small-linear}\\\\\n |\\xi^Te(\\xi)|&\\le C\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]E}\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-diagonal}\\\\\n r|\\mathrm D^2v_i(\\xi,\\xi)|_Q\n       &\\le C\\left(\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]}\\,|\\xi|_H\n                          +\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H^2\\right).\n       \\label{re:field-second}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a fixed $C_v\\ge1$ such that, in every order $m\\ge0$, $$\\label{re:field-jets}\n r_i^{m-1}\\|\\mathrm D^m v_i(P_x)\\|_{H_i(x)\\to Q_i(x)}\n       \\le\\varepsilon C_v^{m+1}(m!)^s.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At $P=P_x$, $x\\in B_1(p)$, write $r=r_i$, $H=H_i(x)$, $Q=Q_i(x)$, $Z=Z_i(x)$, $S=S_i(x)$, and $E=E_i(x)$. There is a linear error $e(\\xi)$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n r^{-1}|v_i|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E,\n       \\label{re:field-value}\\\\\n (\\mathrm Dv_i)\\xi&=S\\xi+e(\\xi),\n       \\label{re:field-linear}\\\\\n |S\\xi|_Q&\\le\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]},\n       &|e(\\xi)|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-small-linear}\\\\\n |\\xi^Te(\\xi)|&\\le C\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]E}\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-diagonal}\\\\\n r|\\mathrm D^2v_i(\\xi,\\xi)|_Q\n       &\\le C\\left(\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]}\\,|\\xi|_H\n                          +\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H^2\\right).\n       \\label{re:field-second}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a fixed $C_v\\ge1$ such that, in every order $m\\ge0$, $$\\label{re:field-jets}\n r_i^{m-1}\\|\\mathrm D^m v_i(P_x)\\|_{H_i(x)\\to Q_i(x)}\n       \\le\\varepsilon C_v^{m+1}(m!)^s.$$",
      "explanation": "Localized smooth covector fields have directional value and derivative bounds, alongside uniform all-order Gevrey estimates.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-re-field-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "At $P=P_x$, $x\\in B_1(p)$, write $r=r_i$, $H=H_i(x)$, $Q=Q_i(x)$, $Z=Z_i(x)$, $S=S_i(x)$, and $E=E_i(x)$. There is a linear error $e(\\xi)$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n r^{-1}|v_i|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E,\n       \\label{re:field-value}\\\\\n (\\mathrm Dv_i)\\xi&=S\\xi+e(\\xi),\n       \\label{re:field-linear}\\\\\n |S\\xi|_Q&\\le\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]},\n       &|e(\\xi)|_Q&\\le C\\sqrt\\varepsilon\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-small-linear}\\\\\n |\\xi^Te(\\xi)|&\\le C\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]E}\\,|\\xi|_H,\n       \\label{re:field-diagonal}\\\\\n r|\\mathrm D^2v_i(\\xi,\\xi)|_Q\n       &\\le C\\left(\\sqrt{Z[\\xi]}\\,|\\xi|_H\n                          +\\sqrt E\\,|\\xi|_H^2\\right).\n       \\label{re:field-second}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a fixed $C_v\\ge1$ such that, in every order $m\\ge0$, $$\\label{re:field-jets}\n r_i^{m-1}\\|\\mathrm D^m v_i(P_x)\\|_{H_i(x)\\to Q_i(x)}\n       \\le\\varepsilon C_v^{m+1}(m!)^s.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\sigma\\ge1$ and put $L_0=1$ and $$L_m=(m+1)((m+1)!)^\\sigma\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$ Then $$\\label{re:convolution-decay}\n \\delta_m:=\\sum_{l=1}^{m-1}\\frac{L_lL_{m-l}}{L_m}\n       \\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{l=0}^m L_lL_{m-l}\\le C_\\sigma L_m.$$ Suppose a smooth matrix-valued function $J$ is invertible at a point and, in Euclidean operator norms at that point, $$\\|J^{-1}\\|\\le\\sqrt A,\\qquad\n \\frac{\\|\\mathrm D^mJ\\|}{m!}\\le B^mL_m\\quad(m\\ge1),\n \\qquad A,B\\ge1.$$ There is a constant $C_{A,\\sigma}$, independent of $m$ and $B$, such that $$\\label{re:inverse-jets}\n \\frac{\\|\\mathrm D^m(J^{-1})\\|}{m!}\n       \\le C_{A,\\sigma}B^mL_m\\qquad(m\\ge0).$$ The assertion also holds for the inverse transpose.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Noncollapsed reference measure** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Throughout, noncollapsed means that the reference measure is exactly $\mathcal H_d^n$.
- **Regular point** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A point is *$n$-regular* if every pointed Gromov–Hausdorff tangent there is pointed-isometric to $(\mathbb R^n,\|\cdot\|,0)$.
- **Tangent** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Here a tangent is a pointed limit of rescalings by inverse radii tending to infinity.
- **Gram matrix** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Write $r_i=2^{-i}$, and let $G=(\langle\nabla u_j,\nabla u_k\rangle)_{j,k=1}^n$ be the Gram matrix field.
- **Bochner matrix measure** ([source](#pm-h-4)): If the rescaled curvature lower bound is $-\kappa$, the matrix-valued Bochner measure is $M=\tfrac12\Delta G+\kappa G\mathfrak m$, with the Laplacian understood entrywise.
- **Local directional mass form** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a fixed enlargement $L>1$, define the positive semidefinite matrix $$D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1} \bigl[r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Lr_i}(y))\bigr]R_i(y)^{-1}, \qquad D[\xi]=\xi^TD\xi.$$
- **Heat and inverse heat Gram matrices** ([source](#stmt-co-heat-matrices)): Define $$\label{co:QH-definition} Q_i(x)=P_{r_i^2}(\chi G)(x)+\lambda r_i^2\mathrm{Id}, \qquad H_i(x)=Q_i(x)^{-1}.$$
- **Shifted Bochner measure** ([source](#pi:shifted-triangle)): For a function with constant Laplacian and a scalar $b$, write $$\mathcal B_\kappa(w,b) =M(w)-(2b\Delta w-nb^2)\mathfrak m. \label{pi:shifted-measure}$$
- **Coordinate value domain** ([source](#re:input-data)): Write $r_i=2^{-i}$, $P_x=u(x)$, and $$\Omega=u(B_1(p)).$$
- **Smooth covector field** ([source](#re:field-definition)): The following is the covector field used in the iteration: $$\label{re:field-definition} v_i(P)=\sum_{y\in\mathcal Y_i} \phi_{i,y}(P)D_{i,y}(P-c_{i,y}).$$
- **Value-space iteration** ([source](#re:iteration)): On the common open set $\Omega$ define, as long as the Jacobians are invertible there, $$\label{re:iteration} h_0(P)=P, \qquad J_i(P)=\mathrm Dh_i(P), \qquad h_{i+1}(P)=h_i(P)+J_i(P)^{-T}v_i(P).$$

          "exact_quote": "For a function with constant Laplacian and a scalar $b$, write $$\\mathcal B_\\kappa(w,b)\n =M(w)-(2b\\Delta w-nb^2)\\mathfrak m.\n \\label{pi:shifted-measure}$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a function with constant Laplacian and a scalar $b$, write $$\\mathcal B_\\kappa(w,b)\n =M(w)-(2b\\Delta w-nb^2)\\mathfrak m.\n \\label{pi:shifted-measure}$$",
      "interpretation": "The shifted measure subtracts the trace contribution associated with the chosen scalar.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Shifted Bochner measure",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"

      "interpretation": "A finite sum of localized affine covectors supplies each update.",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"

      "interpretation": "Each update adds the inverse transpose Jacobian applied to the covector field.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R004** [claim](#stmt-co-flat-setup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-co-flat-setup): Compactness, volume comparison, harmonic approximation, and diagonal extraction. -> Expanding almost Euclidean harmonic input.
- **R005** [claim](#stmt-co-harmonic-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-co-harmonic-normalization): Compactness induction and canonical Gram normalization. -> Positive definite scale Gram matrices and normalized coordinates.
- **R007** [claim](#stmt-co-open-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-co-open-coordinates): Scale separation and successive approximate preimages. -> An open harmonic value domain.
- **R009** [claim](#stmt-co-heat-matrices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-co-heat-matrices): Heat regularization, cutoff-source estimates, and relative tail control. -> Monotone inverse heat forms.
- **R013** [claim](#stmt-co-cumulative-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-co-cumulative-comparison): Quadratic telescoping and normalization-error absorption. -> Cumulative local mass comparison.
- **R015** [claim](#stmt-pi-poisson-pinching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pi-poisson-pinching): Positive-source Dirichlet estimates and telescoping volume drops. -> Summable Poisson defects.
- **R018** [claim](#stmt-pi-radial-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pi-radial-invariance): Affine improvement, exponential convolution, and removal of fit constants. -> Summable radial-invariance errors with positive floors.
- **R021** [claim](#stmt-pi-sharp-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pi-sharp-projection): Radial transport followed by a normalized harmonic compactness contradiction. -> Sharp approximation of the specific two-center potential difference.
- **R022** [claim](#stmt-pi-directional-pinching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pi-directional-pinching): Coefficient recovery, positive-measure polarization, and exact scale conversion. -> Summable directional Bochner control.
- **R026** [claim](#stmt-re-metric-iteration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-re-metric-iteration): Simultaneous finite-scale metric induction and all-order jet bounds. -> Smooth finite iterates with invertible Jacobians and metric bounds.
- **R027** [claim](#stmt-re-limit-chart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-re-limit-chart): Uniform tail convergence, pairwise Taylor comparison, and invariance of domain. -> Open limiting bi-Lipschitz chart using ambient distance.
- **R001** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-re-limit-chart-2): Fix dimension-dependent constants before choosing a sufficiently late rescaling, then scale chart values back. -> The dimension-controlled ambient-open chart.
- **R002** [claim](#stmt-cor-full-measure-atlas) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-full-measure-atlas): Union of chart domains, countable subcover, and composition on overlaps. -> A full-measure open region with countable atlas.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

  "pm_id": "PM-357.1",

  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-co-flat-setup",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-flat-setup",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an $n$-regular point $p$ in the space of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. There are rescalings $$(X_\\nu,d_\\nu,\\mathfrak m_\\nu,p_\\nu)\n =(X,s_\\nu^{-1}d,s_\\nu^{-n}\\mathcal H_d^n,p),\n \\qquad s_\\nu\\downarrow0,$$ numbers $\\kappa_\\nu\\downarrow0$, and radii $T_\\nu\\uparrow\\infty$ with $\\kappa_\\nu T_\\nu^2\\to0$, having the following properties. The space $X_\\nu$ is $\\mathrm{RCD}(-\\kappa_\\nu,n)$. For a sequence $\\delta_\\nu\\downarrow0$, all balls with center in $B_{64T_\\nu}(p_\\nu)$ and radius at most $64T_\\nu$ satisfy $$\\label{co:uniform-flatness}\n \\left|\\frac{\\mathfrak m_\\nu(B_r(y))}{\\omega_n r^n}-1\\right|\n \\le\\delta_\\nu,\n \\qquad\n d_{GH}\\bigl(\\overline B_r(y),\\overline B_r(0^n)\\bigr)\n \\le\\delta_\\nu r.$$ There are harmonic columns $u_\\nu$ on $B_{32T_\\nu}(p_\\nu)$, converging on every fixed ball locally uniformly and strongly in $W^{1,2}$ to Euclidean coordinates, and $$\\label{co:original-gradient-bound}\n |\\nabla u_\\nu|\\le C_n\n \\quad\\text{on }B_{16T_\\nu}(p_\\nu).$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R004",
      "inputs": [
        "Euclidean tangents at the prescribed regular point",
        "Noncollapsed volume convergence"
      ],
      "method": "Compactness, volume comparison, harmonic approximation, and diagonal extraction.",
      "output": "Expanding almost Euclidean harmonic input.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-co-flat-setup"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-co-harmonic-normalization",
      "depends_on": [
        "R004"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-harmonic-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "For every pair of fixed constants $A,C\\ge1$, the matrices $R_i(y)$ are positive definite for $y\\in B_A(p)$ and $i\\ge0$. In the rescaled ball $B_{Cr_i}(y)$, the map $$\\label{co:normalized-column}\n U_{i,y}=\\frac{R_i(y)^{-1/2}(u-u(y))}{r_i}$$ is, after an orthogonal change of target coordinates, an $o(1)$ approximation to Euclidean coordinates, uniformly and strongly in interior $W^{1,2}$. Its gradients are uniformly bounded on each fixed interior enlargement. Its Gram matrix converges strongly in every finite $L^p$ on such enlargements to $\\mathrm{Id}$.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every fixed $C\\ge1$, if $d(y,z)\\le Cr_i$ and $C^{-1}r_i\\le r_j\\le Cr_i$, then $$\\label{co:nearby-R}\n (1-o(1))R_i(y)\\le R_j(z)\\le(1+o(1))R_i(y).$$ For every $a>0$, after choosing a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\\label{co:slow-R}\n C_a^{-1}2^{-2a|i-j|}R_j(y)\n \\le R_i(y)\\le C_a2^{2a|i-j|}R_j(y).$$ The constants are uniform in the center and the two indices. In particular, since $R_0(y)=\\mathrm{Id}+o(1)$ on fixed balls, $$\\label{co:R-lower}\n c_a r_i^{2a}\\mathrm{Id}\\le R_i(y)\\le C\\mathrm{Id}.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R005",

        "Fixed interior enlargements"
      ],

      "output": "Positive definite scale Gram matrices and normalized coordinates.",

      "depends_on": [
        "R005"
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-open-coordinates",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $A\\ge1$, the map $u$ is a homeomorphism from $B_A(p)$ onto an open subset of $\\mathbb R^n$, once the rescaling is sufficiently late. There is a fixed $c>0$ such that, whenever the balls involved stay in a fixed interior working region, $$\\label{co:ellipsoid-containment}\n \\{P:|P-u(x)|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}<c r_i\\}\n \\subset u(B_{r_i/2}(x)).$$ Moreover, for every fixed $D\\ge1$, if $D^{-1}r_i\\le d(x,z)\\le Dr_i$, then $$\\label{co:separation-scale}\n \\bigl||u(z)-u(x)|_{R_i(x)^{-1}}-d(x,z)\\bigr|\n \\le o_D(1)r_i.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-co-heat-matrices",
          "exact_quote": "There is $\\lambda=\\lambda_\\nu>0$, with $\\lambda_\\nu\\to0$, such that, at all fixed testing centers and $0<t\\le1$, $$\\label{co:heat-derivative}\n P_t(\\chi M)\n \\le\\partial_t\\bigl(P_t(\\chi G)+\\lambda t\\mathrm{Id}\\bigr)\n \\le 3P_t(\\chi M)+C\\mathrm{Id}.$$ Define $$\\label{co:QH-definition}\n Q_i(x)=P_{r_i^2}(\\chi G)(x)+\\lambda r_i^2\\mathrm{Id},\n \\qquad H_i(x)=Q_i(x)^{-1}.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n &(1-o(1))R_i(x)\\le Q_i(x)\\le(1+o(1))R_i(x),\n       \\qquad Q_0(x)=\\mathrm{Id}+o(1),\\label{co:QR-comparison}\\\\\n &H_i(x)\\le H_{i+1}(x)\\le(1+o(1))H_i(x).\n       \\label{co:H-monotone}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The nearby-center and slow-growth estimates of Lemma <a href=\"#co:harmonic-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"co:harmonic-normalization\">5</a> hold for $Q_i$ as well. In particular, for every prescribed $a>0$, with a sufficiently late rescaling, $$\\label{co:slow-QH}\n Q_j(x)\\le C_a2^{2a(i-j)}Q_i(x)\\quad(j\\le i),\n \\qquad \\|H_i(x)\\|\\le C_a2^{2ai}.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-357.1"
        }
      ],

        "Normalized harmonic coordinates"

At each center $x\\in B_{A_0}(p)$ and each scale $i\\ge0$, choose at most $J$ centers $y$ with $d(x,y)\\le C_0r_i$. The choice may depend arbitrarily on $x,i$. Set $$\\label{co:D-definition}\n C_{i,y}=r_i^{2-n}M(B_{Lr_i}(y)),\n \\qquad D_{i,y}=R_i(y)^{-1}C_{i,y}R_i(y)^{-1}.$$ Give the chosen centers weights $\\theta_{i,y}\\in[0,1]$, and suppose that for every $i$ at least one has weight one and satisfies $B_{Lr_i}(y)\\supset B_{r_i}(x)$. Write $$\\label{co:ZS-definition}\n Z_i=\\sum_yD_{i,y},\\qquad\n S_i=\\sum_y\\theta_{i,y}D_{i,y}.$$ There is $C_*<\\infty$, depending only on $n,A_0,C_0,L,J$, such that for all sufficiently late rescalings, every such choice, and every integer $k\\ge0$, $$\\label{co:cumulative-estimates}\n Z_i\\le o(1)H_i(x),\\qquad\n \\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}Z_i\\le C_*H_k(x),\\qquad\n H_k(x)\\le C_*\\left(\\mathrm{Id}+\\sum_{i<k}S_i\\right).$$ The $o(1)$ is uniform over the stated centers, all scales, and all choices satisfying these conditions.",

The following bounds hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le q^a-f_i^a&\\le Cr_i^2,\n &\\sup_{B_{4Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla f_i^a|&\\le Cr_i,\n \\label{pi:poisson-boundedness}\\\\\n \\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+2}P_i(a),\n &\\int_{B_{8Sr_i}(a)}|\\nabla(f_i^a-q^a)|\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n &\\le Cr_i^{n+1}P_i(a),\n \\label{pi:poisson-errors}\\\\\n \\mathcal B_\\kappa(f_i^a,1)(B_{2Sr_i}(a))\n &\\le Cr_i^nP_i(a).\n \\label{pi:poisson-shifted-mass}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

If every pointed Gromov–Hausdorff tangent of $X$ at $p$ is pointed-isometric to $(\\mathbb R^n,|\\cdot|,0)$, then there exist an open neighborhood $U$ of $p$, an open set $V\\subset\\mathbb R^n$, and a homeomorphism $F:U\\to V$ such that $$L_n^{-1}d(x,z)\\le |F(x)-F(z)|\\le L_nd(x,z)\n \\qquad (x,z\\in U).$$ The distance on the left and right is the original distance $d$ restricted to $U$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-chart**: Every prescribed regular point admits an ambient-open bi-Lipschitz chart with distortion depending only on dimension.
- **I-ambient**: Chart distance comparisons use the restricted ambient metric.
- **I-open**: The harmonic coordinate domain is open and the harmonic coordinates are a homeomorphism after sufficiently late rescaling.
- **I-iterate**: Each finite value-space iterate is smooth and has an invertible ordinary Jacobian.
- **I-metric**: The Jacobian pullback metric is bounded above and below by the inverse heat Gram form.
- **I-defect**: Metric error control uses the sum of the directional defects without requiring the sum of their square roots.
- **I-frozen**: Derivatives are ordinary derivatives in coordinate value space with frozen norm matrices.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-model**: Apply the chart guarantee to an arbitrary metric model or sampled data. -> The hypotheses needed for the chart guarantee have not been established.
- **A-distance**: Replace the ambient distance by intrinsic path distance inside the chart domain. -> The asserted pairwise bounds concern a different distance.
- **A-radius**: Use a uniform neighborhood radius across spaces and prescribed points. -> The neighborhood may depend on the space and point.
- **A-regular-locus**: Identify the full-measure open chart region with an open exact regular locus. -> The atlas region can include points outside the exact regular locus.
- **A-analytic-regularity**: Treat the limiting chart as having a Hölder continuous metric tensor or bounded component Laplacians. -> These stronger analytic regularity properties are not asserted.
- **A-matrices**: Require the scale metrics to converge or differentiate them during the iteration. -> This changes the stated analytic inputs and derivative regime.
- **A-global-iterate**: Assume each finite iterate is a globally injective chart. -> Jacobian invertibility does not supply the asserted global injectivity.
- **A-finite-certificate**: Certify the chart theorem by checking finitely many matrices or scales. -> Finite checks do not establish the infinite limit and its all-pairs metric bounds.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The neighborhood chart is obtained after sufficiently late rescaling as a uniform limit over all dyadic scales. The patch centers are chosen using infima of directional defects, and harmonic, Poisson and heat data are assumed from the geometric construction. No finite input representation, terminating chart algorithm or computable stopping threshold is supplied. Finite matrix identities are auxiliary proof steps and do not decide the chart conclusion.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The chart existence theorem supplies no software interface or finite approximation contract. A software implementation would need a separately justified discretisation and error certificate. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local chart theorem concerns an exact noncollapsed metric measure space. It supplies no simulation update, collision model, or guarantee for sampled geometry. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The harmonic and heat constructions require geometric and analytic input not supplied by an executable tool contract. A coordinate tool would need certified input and approximation guarantees. |
| algorithms | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The value-space update suggests an analytic renormalisation pattern: use the inverse transpose Jacobian to convert directional matrix information into metric increments. Algorithmic relevance is inspirational; the chart conclusion uses an infinite limit without an effective stopping rule. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive matrix fields organise a continuous proof. No discrete storage representation or data-structure operation is specified. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Matrix operations occur inside a continuous geometric construction. The paper supplies no accelerator architecture, arithmetic specification, or parallel execution guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The metric comparison theorem provides no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The chart theorem assumes exact geometric regularity and noncollapse. It supplies no estimator or guarantee that finite observations establish these hypotheses. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Patch centers are selected using positive defect floors, but the paper supplies no finite optimisation problem or certified procedure for computing these defects. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction controls geometric scale metrics. It supplies no communication, consistency, or distributed execution contract. |
| other (Metric geometry and geometric analysis) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper establishes an ambient-open bi-Lipschitz chart at every prescribed regular point of a noncollapsed space with the stated synthetic curvature bound. This is a direct geometric application; no effective numerical chart construction is supplied. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L001** (hypothesis): The chart theorem requires the stated noncollapsed curvature-dimension setting and Euclidean tangents at the prescribed point. Blocked use: ['Extending the pointwise chart guarantee to collapsed spaces or points whose regularity is unproved.']
- **L002** (hypothesis): Noncollapse fixes the reference measure to the Hausdorff measure of the ambient metric. Blocked use: ['Replacing the reference measure by an arbitrary weighted measure while retaining the theorem guarantee.']
- **L003** (formal_scope): The distance comparisons use the original ambient distance restricted to the chart domain. Blocked use: ['Substituting intrinsic path distance or a sampled distance without a separate comparison.']
- **L004** (edge_case): The neighborhood may depend on the space and the point; dimensional control of distortion does not give a uniform neighborhood radius. Blocked use: ['Inferring a dimension-only lower bound on the chart radius.']
- **L005** (formal_scope): The full-measure open chart region need not coincide with the exact regular locus, whose openness is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Identifying the chart region with the exact regular locus.']
- **L006** (counterexample_scope): The chart conclusion does not assert Hölder continuity of its metric tensor. Blocked use: ['Promoting the chart to coordinates with a Hölder continuous metric tensor.']
- **L007** (formal_scope): The limiting coordinate components are not asserted to have bounded Laplacian. Blocked use: ['Applying regular-map results that require bounded Laplacians to the limiting coordinates without additional proof.']
- **L008** (approximation): Open harmonic coordinates require a sufficiently late rescaling after fixed enlargements have been chosen. Blocked use: ['Claiming the harmonic map is an open homeomorphism at an arbitrary rescaling.']
- **L009** (edge_case): Scale-dependent Gram matrices may degenerate as the scale tends to zero. Blocked use: ['Assuming a uniform positive lower eigenvalue for the original harmonic Gram matrices at all scales.']
- **L010** (formal_scope): The construction does not differentiate the scale metrics, assume their finite limit, or require global injectivity of finite iterates. Blocked use: ['Differentiating the scale metrics or using global inverses of finite iterates as if guaranteed.']
- **L011** (hypothesis): Reverse cumulative comparison requires a weight-one center whose mass ball covers the testing ball at every scale. Blocked use: ['Dropping the covering-center requirement while retaining the reverse cumulative bound.']
- **L012** (hypothesis): Radial transport requires boundedness and the stated radial equation with controlled affine forcing. Blocked use: ['Applying the expanded-ball bound to arbitrary Sobolev residuals.']
- **L013** (nonconstructive): The limiting chart uses an infinite scale limit and a sufficiently flat regime; a certified finite chart construction and stopping rule are not supplied. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite matrix diagnostic or a truncated update as a certificate of the chart theorem.']
